§ 34.09 GRANT APPLICATION PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Prior to grant application.
      (1)   The county grant application must be completed and signed by office
holder or department head, which request for grant application is attached to
Commissioner Resolution 2014-18 and incorporated by reference as if set forth
in full herein.
      (2)   An application submitted by an organization(s) or individual(s)
other than a county department must be completed and signed by the appropriate
person(s) in charge,
      (3)   The application, as well as the official grant request, must be
delivered to the County Auditor to be placed on the meeting agendas of the
County Council and the Board of Commissioners not less than seven days prior to
meetings, in addition to any grant documentation.
      (4)   If the grant requires a budget, the budget must also be presented
with the request application.
      (5)   The grant application will be considered by the County Council and
the Board of Commissioners.
      (6)   The Board of Commissioners shall approve or deny applications for
all grants for supplies and other services and charges. The County Council and
Board of Commissioners shall approve or deny applications for all grants for
personal service.
      (7)   Note: For notice and payments, the mailing address for the grant
application is: Randolph County Auditor, Courthouse, 100 S. Main Street,
Winchester, Indiana 47394.
   (B)   After grant application.
      (1)   If the grant is awarded to the county and/or applicant, a copy of
the award letter and all other documents received must be provided to the
Auditor's office.
      (2)   Any communication, correspondence and/or changes made between the
applicant and grantor must be provided to the Auditor's office. No exceptions
to this requirement.
      (3)   It is the responsibility of the county department or applicant to
comply with grantor's reporting requirements. Final reports will be given to
the Auditor.
   (C)   Any department, organization or individual which fails to comply with
these requirements may be denied grant approval.
(Comm. Res. 2014-18, passed 12-2-2014)

§ 34.13 BROADBAND READY COMMUNITY.
   (A)   Definitions. As used in this chapter:
      PERMIT. Any local permit, license, certificate, approval, registration,
or similar form of approval required by policy, administrative rule,
regulation, ordinance, or resolution with respect to a project.
      PROJECT. The construction or deployment of wireline or wireless
communications facilities to provide communications services (as defined in
I.C. 8-1-32.5-3) in a unit.
   (B)   Notwithstanding any other provision of this section, regulation,
policy or practice, the following shall apply to a project:
      Randolph County Board of Commissioners shall:
      (1)   Appoint a single point of contact for all matters related to a
project;
      (2)   Establish procedures to allow all forms, applications,
documentation related to a project to be filed or submitted and signed by
electronic means;
      (3)   Review and approve or reject all applications for a permit related
to a project within ten business days after an application is filed or
submitted; and
      (4)   Assure that after an application is approved pursuant to division
(B)(3), any inspections, including any additional necessary approvals, related
to a project will occur in a timely and expeditious manner.
   (C)   Randolph County shall adopt adequate processes and procedures to
implement the provisions of division (B). Processes and procedures established
hereunder may not do the following:
      (1)   Require an application to designate a final contractor to complete
a project;
      (2)   Impose a fee to review an application or issue a permit for a
project;
      (3)   Impose a seasonal moratorium on the issuance of permits for a
project; or
      (4)   Discriminate among communications service providers or utilities
with respect to any action described in this section or otherwise related to a
project, including granting access to public rights-of-way, infrastructure and
poles, river and bridge crossings, and any other physical assets owned or
controlled by Randolph County.
(Comm. Ord. 2020-10, passed 10-5-2020)
§ 34.14 ELECTRONIC DEVICES PROHIBITED ON THIRD FLOOR OF COUNTY COURTHOUSE.
   (A)   No person may bring onto the third floor of the Randolph County
Courthouse an electronic device, including, but not limited to, a cellular
telephone, camera, video camera, any device capable of audio and/or video
recording, PDA's, iPods, pagers, and any device that has Internet capabilities,
except for the following:
      (1)   Authorized officers of the court, including but not limited to,
judges, attorneys, probation officers, case managers, CASAs, and guardians ad
litem.
      (2)   Any county employee, or appointed or elected officials, who are on
the third floor of the county courthouse for county business.
      (3)   Any other individual whom a judge authorizes to bring in such a
device.
      (4)   Cameras may be allowed for ceremonial purposes, as specifically
authorized by each court, which includes but is not limited to, educational
functions, weddings, and final adoptions.
   (B)   The Sheriff of Randolph County and court personnel are authorized to
monitor and enforce compliance with this section.
   (C)   This section does not prohibit anyone with a disability that
necessitates the availability or use of an electronic device to possess one on
the third floor of the county courthouse.
   (D)   A person is not prohibited from bringing an electronic device to
record any public meeting held on the third floor of the county courthouse. The
person may only bring that electronic device onto the third floor of the
courthouse to record the public meeting and must turn off that electronic
device after the public meeting has concluded. The person may only record the
public meeting and may not record anywhere else on the third floor of the
county courthouse.
   (E)   A person who violates any provision of this section shall be fined not
more than $2,500 for each offense.
(Comm. Ord. 2022-29, passed 11-21-2022)

CHAPTER 51: TELEPHONE SYSTEM
Section
   
51.01   E911 Advisory Committee
   
51.02   E911 fee
§ 51.01 E911 ADVISORY COMMITTEE.
   (A)   The terms ENHANCED EMERGENCY TELEPHONE SYSTEM, SERVICE SUPPLIER and
SERVICE USER shall have the meanings assigned by state law.
   (B)   A County E911 Advisory Committee (the “E911 Advisory Committee”)
hereby is created for the purpose of providing the County Board of
Commissioners with information, advice and recommendations regarding the
implementation, operation and maintenance of the enhanced emergency telephone
system, including, without limitation, the training of personnel, dispatch
procedures and standards, communications facilities and systems, record keeping
and analysis of the quality and timeliness of system and dispatch service,
review of fees and expenses, recommendation as to purchases and other matters
which from time to time shall be assigned for review to the Committee by the
County Board of Commissioners.
   (C)   (1)   The E911 Advisory Committee shall be composed of eight members
and shall be made up of the following, each with an initial term extending from
the effective date of this section through the date indicated:
         (a)   One member to be designated by the City Council of the City of
Winchester, with expiration of initial term on 12-31-1996;
         (b)   One member to be designated by the City Council of the City of
Union City, with expiration of initial term on 12-31-1995;
         (c)   The County Sheriff or a member to be designated by the County
Sheriff, with expiration of initial term on 12-31-1994;
         (d)   One member (not living in Winchester or Union City nor
associated with the fire Departments of those cities) to be designated by the
Chiefs of the Fire Departments in the county, with expiration of initial term
on 12-31-1996;
         (e)   One member (likewise not living in Winchester or Union City nor
associated with the police departments of those cities) to be designated by the
Chiefs of Police and Town Marshals in the county, with expiration of initial
term on 12-31-1995;
         (f)   One member to be designated by the County Board of Commissioners
from the paid staff of the County EMS agency or from the paid staff of any
ambulance service having an operations base located in the county, with
expiration of initial term on 12-31-1994;
         (g)   One member of the County Board of Commissioners or a member to
be designated by the County Board of Commissioners, with expiration of initial
term on 12-31-1994, and thereafter to be designated on an annual basis; and
         (h)   One member of the County Council or a member to be designated by
the County Council, with expiration of initial term on 12-31-1994, and
thereafter to be designated on an annual basis.
      (2)   Prior to expiration of the initial term of each member of the E911
Advisory Committee, a successor shall be designated in the same manner for a
term of three calendar years, with the exception of those members for which
annual terms are specified. Vacancies shall be filled in the same manner for
the remainder of the current term.
   (D)   The E911 Advisory Committee shall meet no less frequently than
quarterly, with an initial organizational meeting to be held within 60 days
after the effective date of this section, and subsequent organizational
meetings to be held in January or February of each year, at a time and location
to be set by public notice published by the County Board of Commissioners.
Copies of the notice of organizational meeting shall be provided by the County
Board of Commissioners to all entities responsible for designating members to
the E911 Advisory Committee. Designations of members shall be reported to
County Board of Commissioners by notice to County Auditor.
   (E)   The selection of the members to be designated by the Fire Chiefs and
Chiefs of Police shall be accomplished by vote of each group, conducted in
meeting or by mail or other means of communication. If the designation is not
reported prior to or at the applicable organizational meeting of the E911
Advisory Committee, the County Board of Commissioners shall make the
designation.
   (F)   The E911 Advisory Committee shall select a Chairperson and a Secretary
by majority vote at its annual organizational meeting. Notice of meetings of
the E911 Advisory Committee shall be made public and in compliance with the
State Open Door Act. Minutes of the meetings of the E911 Advisory Committee
shall be kept and a copy of all minutes adopted shall be referred to the County
Board of Commissioners.
   (G)   The County Board of Commissioners shall appoint a director of the
county enhanced emergency telephone system (the “E911 Director”), who shall
report to the Board of Commissioners and shall work with the E911 Advisory
Committee, with agencies and offices of and in the county, and with service
suppliers to manage, coordinate and maintain the county enhanced emergency
telephone system, including, without limitation, the training of personnel,
dispatch procedures and standards, communications facilities and systems,
record keeping and analysis of the quality and timeliness of system and
dispatch service, review of fees and expenses, recommendation as to purchases
and other matters.
   (H)   The Building Commissioner of the county shall be delegated the
authority to work with agencies and offices of and in the county and with
service suppliers to control, coordinate and maintain the countywide address
numbering system and associated data base employed in support of the county
enhanced emergency telephone system, with the address numbering system to be
codified under an ordinance of the County Board of Commissioners. The Building
Commissioner shall report to and consult with the County Board of
Commissioners, with the E911 Advisory Committee and with the E911 Director with
regard to the countywide address numbering system.
   (I)   The 50 service users, more or less, located in the county in the
Indiana Bell Telephone Albany exchange and served by an enhanced emergency
telephone system provided by and from Delaware County, shall not now be
included in the county enhanced emergency telephone system, but shall remain
under the existing arrangement for service from Delaware County.
   (J)   The collection by service suppliers of the enhanced emergency
telephone system fee established under §
51.02 shall begin on 1-1-1994.
(Comm. Ord. 93-20, passed 11-8-1993)
Cross-reference:
   E911 Advisory Committee responsibilities as to street naming and addressing,
see §
93.07
§ 51.02 E911 FEE.
   Under the authority of and subject to state law, a monthly enhanced
emergency telephone system fee hereby is established for the funding of an
enhanced emergency telephone system to serve the county. This fee shall be
collected from the telephone service users of the county and administered in
accordance with the following terms.
   (A)   A monthly fee of$2.50 shall be collected by each service supplier from
each service user to whom it provides exchange telephone service within the
county, except as otherwise may be provided under division (B) below.
   (B)   Approximately 50, more or less, service users, located in the county
in the Indiana Bell Telephone Albany exchange have been and are served by an
enhanced emergency telephone system provided by and from Delaware County as is
permitted under state law, and as that arrangement was authorized and in
operation prior to the adoption of this section to provide an enhanced
emergency telephone system in the county, it shall be the responsibility of the
County Board of Commissioners to determine whether to include the service users
in the county enhanced emergency telephone system or whether to leave in effect
the preexisting arrangement for service from Delaware County, giving due
consideration to the costs of service, the effectiveness of service and the
procedures necessary to rescind or amend the preexisting arrangement with
Delaware County.
   (C)   Pursuant to state law, each service supplier that collects the
enhanced emergency telephone system fee on behalf of the county is entitled to
retain a 4% administration fee as compensation for collecting the fees.
   (D)   The collection of fees shall begin on the first day of a month to be
determined by resolution of the County Board of Commissioners, not earlier than
the effective date as provided by state law, with a minimum of 30 days notice
to service providers. The fees shall be collected by service providers
according to the procedure set out by state law, and shall be remitted to the
County Treasurer and reported to the County Auditor as provided by state law.
The County Treasurer shall deposit the fees in a separate fund denominated the
County Emergency Telephone System Fund, which may be invested in the same
manner in which other money of the county may be invested, and the County
Treasurer shall deposit any income earned from the investment in the County
Emergency Telephone System Fund, all in accord with state law.
(Council Ord. 93-16, passed 11-2-1993; Council Ord. 2010-01, passed 1-5-2010)
CHAPTER 52: SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEMS

§ 52.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ADMINISTRATIVE AUTHORITY. The County Board of Health.
   BUILDING DRAIN. The part of the lowest horizontal piping of a house drainage
system which receives the discharge from soil, waste and other drainage pipes
inside the walls of any building and conveys the same to the building’s sewer
beginning two feet outside the building.
   BUILDING SEWER. The part of the horizontal piping from the end of the
building drain to its connection with the main sewer or private sewage disposal
system and conveying the drainage of but one building site.
   BULLETIN NO. S.E. 11. State Department of Health Bulletin No. S.E. 11
entitled The Sanitary Privy, as updated or amended from time to time, and any
bulletin or publication which may hereafter be published by the State
Department of Health as a supplement or successor to the Bulletin No. S.E. 11,
and of any publication shall be on file in the office of the County Health
Officer for public inspection during regular business hours.
   DWELLING. Any house or place used or intended to be used as a place of
residence.
   FOUNDATION DRAIN. The portion of a building drainage system provided to
drain ground water from the outside of the foundation or under basement floor,
not including any sewage.
   INDIANA STATE DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH RULE 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1. The State
Department of Health Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1, Residential Sewage Disposal
Systems, as updated from time to time, and any bulletin or publication which
may hereafter be published by the State Department of Health as a supplement or
successor to State Department of Health Rule 410 IAC 6-8.1, Residential Sewage
Disposal Systems. Copies of the State Department of Health Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-
8.1, Residential Sewage Disposal Systems, and any supplemental or successor
publication shall be on file in the office of the County Health Officer for
public inspection during regular business hours.
   INDIANA STATE DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH RULE 410 I.A.C. 6-10, ENTITLED COMMERCIAL
ON-SITE WASTEWATER DISPOSAL. Updated or amended from time to time, and any
bulletin or publication which may hereafter be published by the State
Department of Health as a supplement of successor to the Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-10.
Copies of the Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-10 and of any supplemental or successor
publication shall be on file in the office or the County Health Officer for
public inspection during regular business hours.
   SEWAGE. Any combination of human excreta and/or grey water from water
closets, laundries, sinks, dishwashers, bathing facilities, septic tank
effluent and other objectionable waste waters.
   SOIL SURVEY. The book entitled Soil Survey of Randolph County, Indiana,
published by the United States Department of Agriculture, Soil Conservation
Service. This information is available through the County Soil Conservation
Service for public inspection during normal office hours.
   SUBDIVISION. The division of a parcel of land shown as a unit or as
contiguous units created for the purpose of transfer of building development.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.02 GENERAL REQUIREMENTS OF PRIVATE SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEMS.
   (A)   Where a public sanitary sewer is not available within 300 feet of the
property line in the county, all persons owning or leasing property shall
comply with the following provisions of this chapter for private sewage
disposal systems.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to place, deposit or permit to be
deposited in an unsanitary manner upon any public or private property, river,
stream or ditch within the county or in any area under the jurisdiction of the
county, any human excrement, sewage or overflow drainage and/or contents of
septic tanks.
   (C)   At any public, commercial or business building situated within the
county where no public sewer system is available and where private sewage
disposal systems may be approvable those private sewage disposal systems shall
be installed, constructed and maintained in compliance with the minimum
standards of State Department of Health (Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-10). Plans and
specifications shall be submitted to the State Department or Health and
Superior Boards where required for their examination and approval. Final
acceptance and inspection of the system shall be granted at the discretion of
the County Health Officer.
   (D)   The design, construction, installation, location, maintenance and
operation of residential sewage disposal systems shall comply with the
provisions of this regulation.
   (E)   Any privy situated with the county shall be of the sanitary type and
shall be installed, constructed, maintained and disposed of in accordance with
the standards of the State Department of Health. All privies shall be kept in a
clean condition so that insects and rodents do not enter the vault and shall be
located the protect the water supplies from contamination.
   (F)   All private residential sewage disposal systems shall be installed,
constructed and maintained in a manner accepted by the County Health Officer
and in compliance with the minimum standards of the State Department of Health.
   (G)   Should any defect, malfunction or noncompliance exist, or occur, in
any private sewage disposal system or privy which would cause the sewage
disposal system or privy to fail to meet the standards herein required, the
defect or malfunction shall be corrected without delay and in the shortest
reasonable time by the owner of the premises or his, her or their agents in a
manner as shall satisfy the County Health Officer. Failure of the owner or
occupant to do so shall constitute a violation of this chapter. The violator
shall be subject to the penalties prescribed of this chapter.
   (H)   Wherever a public combined or sanitary sewer or community sanitary
sewer, approved by the State Department of Health, become available and is
within 300 feet or the property line of the residential or business property
served by a private sewage disposal system or privy, situated within the
county, a direct connection shall be made to the sewer (provided that, the
sewer is of sufficient capacity) and any septic tank, sewage pits, outhouses,
privy pits and similar sewage disposal and treatment facilities shall be
abandoned and filled in a safe and sanitary manner as approved by the County
Health Officer. The direct connections shall be completed by the owner and/or
the occupant of the premises or his, her or their agents within 90 days after
receipt of written notice ordering the action. Failure of the owner or the
occupant to comply with the provisions of this section shall constitute a
violation of this chapter and the violation shall be subject to the penalties
prescribed of this chapter.
   (I)   Not withstanding any other provision of this chapter, where a
residence or business building is constructed, within the county which will
utilize a sewage disposal system, each building site shall consist of an area
not less than one acre (43,560 square feet) and an appropriate amount of
undisturbed soil area set aside for a sewage disposal system, where public
sewers are not available to serve a residence or business building. Sewage
therefor shall be disposed of in accordance with this chapter.
   (J)   Not withstanding any other provision of this chapter, the County
Health Officer may limit or prohibit the use of a sewage disposal system for
residences or businesses in areas that are seasonally wet, ponds water or which
periodically floods during any part of the year, or in areas where the size or
topography of the site, or where information contained in the soil survey or
obtained from site specific soil analysis conducted or properly supervised by
the County Health Officer indicates a reasonable opinion that a sewage disposal
system will not function properly and will dispose of sewage in an unsanitary
manner in violation of the terms of this chapter.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.03 MATERIALS AND LOCATIONS.
   (A)   Septic tanks shall be located in accordance with the following
distances and shall be of appropriate construction, strength and capacity as
required by the State Department of Health:
Minimum Distance in Feet From - To Septic Tank Absorption System
Minimum Distance in Feet From - To     Septic Tank Absorption System
Dwelling or other structure            10          10
Lake or reservoir                      50          50
Private water supply source            50          50
Public water supply source             200         200
Semi-public water supply source        100         100
Side or rear lot line                  5           5
Stream, ditch or drainage tile         25          25
Suction water lines                    50          50
Water lines continually under pressure 10          10
 
   (B)   Connecting pipes that connect septic tanks installed in series shall
be four inches minimum diameter. All inlet and outlet connections at the septic
tank shall be sealed with an appropriate material. All joints in the sewer
connecting septic tanks in series shall be water tight.
   (C)   Distribution boxes or series of distribution boxes shall be installed
between the septic tank and subsurface absorption system and each absorption
system line shall directly thereto. It shall be installed in a manner that it
will remain level and provide equal distribution of flow to the individual
lines of the subsurface absorption system. The pipe connection between septic
tank and the distribution box and the pipe connecting the distribution box and
subsurface absorption system must be water tight and be constructed of
appropriate material as required by the State Department of Health. The
distribution box shall be constructed of water tight, impervious material. The
distribution box shall be provided with an opening such as a ready access for
inspection and general maintenance.
   (D)   Absorption system pipe shall meet the specifications of the State
Department of Health.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.04 CONSTRUCTION OF SUBSURFACE ABSORPTION FIELD.
   (A)   All subsurface absorption fields shall be located in accordance with
distances set forth in §
52.03(A). Minimum trench depth is 18 inches, with a maximum trench width of 36
inches. Minimum trench depth shall be ten inches and a maximum trench depth of
36 inches. Soil cover of 12 inches minimum from the top of the tile.
   (B)   The approved absorption tile or perforated plastic pipe shall be
completed surrounded by coarse gravel, stone or other approved material at
least six inches below the tile or pipe and extend upward to at least two
inches above the tile or pipe. The top of the stone shall be covered with
geotextile fabric or six inches of compacted straw.
   (C)   The aggregate or other approved materials shall be ranging in size
from one inch to two and one-half inches. Fines, dust, sand and clay must be
removed from the material before being placed in the trench.
   (D)   Absorption lines shall be individually connected to a distribution box
to ensure equal distribution to the entire field.
   (E)   Subsurface absorption fields shall not be constructed in areas where
the land surface gradient is greater than 12%. All trenches shall be level for
the entire length of the trench. No single trench shall exceed 100 feet in
length. There shall be a minimum separation of seven and one-half feet center
to center between the absorption field trenches.
   (F)   Gravelless pipe systems may be installed in conditions and sites for
specifications set forth by the State Department of Health.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.05 SOIL ANALYSIS REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Properties of the soil profile of each site shall be evaluated using
guidelines as set forth in the soil manuals and handbooks of the Soil
Conservation Service, U.S. Department of Agriculture and where necessary by
site soil analysis conducted in accordance with State Department of Health Rule
410 I.A.C. 6-8.1.
   (B)   Subsurface absorption fields shall not be constructed in soils rated
as having severe or very severe limitations for subsurface sewage disposal by
the Soil Conservation Service, U.S. Department of Agriculture unless that
limitation is not present as shown by field investigation or can be overcome.
   (C)   Subsurface absorption fields shall not be constructed in soils in
which seasonable high water table, bedrock, glacial till, poor filter or other
limiting layers exist within two feet of proposed trench bottom unless the
system is installed to overcome the restrictions as required in State
Department of Health Rule 410 I.A.C. 6-8.1.
   (D)   No subsurface absorption field shall be installed in unsuitable soils
such as unconsolidated fill. Roof, foundation and storm water drains shall not
discharge into nor upon the system. There shall be no construction of any kind,
including driveways, covering any portion of a residential sewage disposal
system. The connecting sewers between the house and septic tank, the septic
tank and distribution box and the distribution box and the absorption lines may
installed under driveways if the sewer is constructed of cast iron.
   (E)   Data for determining loading rates for absorption trenches using site
specific soil analysis information. See Table titled “Loading Rates for
Subsurface Systems”. This table will be used in determining size of absorption
field based on calculations set forth in State Department of Health Rule 410
I.A.C. 6-8.1.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.06 PERMITS TO INSTALL, PERMIT FEES AND INSPECTIONS; REGISTRATION OF
INSTALLERS.
   (A)   Before commencement of construction, repair or alteration of any
public, commercial or business or new private sewage disposal system or privy
is to be installed the owner shall first obtain a written permit signed by the
County Health Officer. The application shall be supplemented by any plans,
specifications or information deemed necessary by the Health Officer. A permit
fee of $25 shall be paid to the County Health Officer for deposit in the County
Health Fund at the time the application is filed.
   (B)   A repair permit shall be necessary for any property where sewage is
found on the ground’s surface or discharging off-lot and is traceable by means
of dye-testing or having a bacteria water test.
   (C)   The County Health Officer shall examine the application. Together with
all information accompanying the same and if he or she determines, consistent
with the provisions of this chapter, that the application should be approved,
he or she shall issue the permit. Otherwise, he or she shall deny the
application and shall notify the applicant in writing of the reason or reasons
for such. An application shall be denied if information submitted is inaccurate
or incomplete. The permit, if issued, shall be posted prior to commencement of
construction in a conspicuous place at or near the building where the sewage
disposal system is under construction. The permit shall be plainly visible from
the public thoroughfare serving the building.
   (D)   If the sewage disposal system for which the permit was issued has not
been constructed, installed or extended within one year from the date of
issuance, the permit shall automatically expire and become void.
   (E)   A permit for the construction or installation of any private sewage
disposal system or privy shall not constitute final approval of the system
until the same is completed to the satisfaction of the County Health Officer.
He or she, or his or her agent, shall be permitted to inspect the work at any
stage of construction, and in any event, before any underground portions are
covered, the applicant for the permit shall notify the County Health Officer.
The final inspection shall be made within the shortest reasonable time, but
shall not exceed two working days of receipt of notice, excluding weekends or
legal holidays, providing the Health Officer is notified 48 hours before
inspection is necessary. Notification may be telephoned, written or in person.
   (F)   The County Health Officer shall maintain a register of all persons
engaged in, or intended to engage, in the installation, construction or repair
of sewage disposal systems or equipment within the county, who contracts
services for hire.
   (G)   Any person engaged in, or intending to engage in contracting for hire,
the installation and construction, repair, replacement or alteration of sewage
disposal systems or equipment shall make application to the County Health
Officer to have his or her name placed on the registration for those engaged in
contracting for hire, the installation, construction and repair of sewage
disposal systems and equipment. The application form shall contain the name and
address of the firm or place of business the person is associated with and
other information as the County Health Officer may reasonably require to aid
him or her in the administration and enforcement of these regulations, or to
help him or her determine whether there is any reason why he or she should not
approve the application.
   (H)   Any person making application to have his or her name placed on the
registration for those engaged in the installation, construction and repair of
sewage disposal systems or equipment shall submit with the application a fee of
$20 per calendar year or $10 after June 30 for any part less than six months
thereof. Only one application need be submitted, pursuant to divisions (F) and
(G) above, if the person is a firm, partnership, association or corporation.
   (I)   The County Health Officer may remove the name of any person from the
list of registration who have demonstrated inability or unwillingness to comply
with these regulations. The person may have his or her name reinstated on the
list by the County Board of Health after satisfactory demonstration of ability
or willingness to comply with these regulations.
   (J)   All fees collected under terms of this chapter shall be receipted into
the County Health Fund for services rendered in enforcing this chapter.
   (K)   Failure of any person to obtain a permit or to register or to
otherwise comply with the requirements of this section shall constitute a
violation of this chapter and violators shall be subject to the penalties
herein prescribed.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.07 POWER FOR INSPECTION.
   (A)   The County Health Officer or his or her agent bearing proper
credentials and identification shall be permitted to enter upon all properties
subject to the provisions of this chapter at reasonable times for purposes of
inspections, observations, measurements, sampling and testing necessary to
carry out the provisions of this chapter.
   (B)   The County Health Officer or his or her agent may compel the immediate
suspension of any work, completed or in process or planned that is in violation
of any provisions of this chapter. The suspension order shall be posted at the
construction site and confirmed by written notice to the owner or occupant of
the premises and any other person engaged in the performance of the work or any
person causing the work to be done. Failure to comply with the suspension order
shall constitute a violation of this chapter and the violator shall be subject
to the penalties prescribed in this chapter.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.08 NOTICE.
   Any person in violation of any provisions of this chapter shall be served
with written notice by the County Health Officer stating the nature of the
violation and providing a time limit for compliance.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.09 APPEAL.
   (A)   Any person aggrieved by any action or decision of the County Health
Officer may appeal by filing a request in writing with the County Board of
Health not less than 30 days following the action of the Health Officer unless
appellant obtains an extension of time in writing from the County Board of
Health; and shall specify the adverse decision appealed from or from which an
extension is requested and the grounds therefor.
   (B)   The County Health Officer may require the appellant to provide any
needed information by the County Board of Health in order to render a
satisfactory judgement.
   (C)   The County Health Officer shall forthwith transmit to the County Board
of Health all of the papers in his or her office, constituting the record of
the case.
   (D)   The County Board of Health, upon receipt of the notice and record,
shall immediately select a reasonable time and place for the hearing and give
notice or the time and place thereof to the appellant in writing. The hearing
of the appeal may be continued from time to time, but following the conclusion
of the hearing, a decision on the appeal shall be rendered by the Board within
20 days thereafter. Any person may appear and testify at the hearing, either in
person or by counsel.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.10 EFFECTIVE DATE.
   This chapter shall take effect from a day after the date of its passage.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)
§ 52.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of
this chapter shall be guilty of an infraction. On conviction, the violator
shall be punished by a fine of not more than $2,500, and each day after the
expiration of the time limit for abating insanitary conditions and completing
improvements to abate the conditions as ordered by the County Board of Health,
or by the duly appointed representative of the County Board of Health, shall
constitute a distinct and separate offense.
   (B)   The County Board of Health shall also be entitled to seek any other
legal remedy available against any person, firm or corporation who shall
violate any provisions of this chapter.
(Comm. Ord. 98-15, passed 8-31-1998)

70.02   Vehicle weight restrictions
   
70.03   Use of off-road vehicles
 
   
70.99   Penalty
§ 70.01 TRAVEL STATUS CLASSIFICATIONS; EMERGENCIES.
   (A)   The Board of County Commissioners may declare one of the three
declarations or emergencies set out in division (B) below.
   (B)   There shall be three classifications of travel situations or
catastrophes to control county highway use:
      (1)   Travel warning. A state of emergency has been declared by the Board
of County Commissioners. Travel is restricted to emergency personnel. All
county roadways are closed, and no one should be out during these conditions.
No non-emergency vehicle(s) shall be on county roads. Further restrictions may
be included in the declaration.
      (2)   Travel watch. Travel conditions have developed that are threatening
to the safety of the public. Only essential travel is recommended (i.e., to and
from work, for medical emergencies, home necessities, or other emergency
situations). Emergency action plans have been or should be implemented by
businesses, schools, government agencies and other organizations.
      (3)   Travel advisory. Routine travel or activities may be restricted in
areas because of a hazardous situation (i.e. blowing and drifting snow, icy
roadways, flooding, downed trees or poles, and the like). Citizens should drive
with caution or avoid these areas. Schools and businesses may begin to
implement their emergency action plans.
   (C)   The county will not be held liable for any damage done to any vehicle
that has impaired the clearing of any county roadway.
   (D)   The EMA Director shall be the designee of the President of the Board
of County Commissioners to change the county travel status if contact with any
Commissioner cannot be established by telephone for changing the county travel
status.
(Comm. Ord. 2004-18, passed 8-16-2004; Comm. Ord. 2010-02, passed 1-18-2010;
Comm. Ord. 2010-04, passed 2-12-2010; Comm. Ord. 2013-20, passed 12-2-2013)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.02 VEHICLE WEIGHT RESTRICTIONS.
   (A)   Until and subject to further order of the County Board of
Commissioners, during intermittent times during a period commencing February 1,
and continuing through April 1, for all calendar years hereafter, the gross
weight of vehicles operated on county roadways are limited, as follows, except
on the award of written permit by the County Highway Superintendent when the
Frost Law has been declared in effect:
 
Rim Width     2-tire axle 4-tire axle
4.5" - 7.0"   5,200 lbs.  11,000 lbs.
7.5" - 9.0"   7,200lbs.   11,500 lbs.
9.75" - 13.0" 8,000 lbs.  11,500 lbs.
14.0" - 15.0" 10,000 lbs. 11,500 lbs.
 
   (B)   It shall be in the sole and absolute discretion of the County Board of
Commissioners to declare when the Frost Law is in effect.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful to operate or cause to be operated upon any
public road or public highway of the county highway system, excepting therefrom
any roads or highways that are included in the state highway system, any
vehicle or combination of vehicles having a maximum gross axle weight, with
load, in excess of the axle weights as described in division (A).
   (D)   Vehicles with a maximum gross weight, with load, as described in
division (A), must first obtain a special written permit from the County
Highway Superintendent. The permit shall be issued in triplicate, with one copy
being retained in the Highway Superintendent's office; one copy being retained
in the Sheriff’s office; and one copy being carried in the vehicle containing
the overload or retained in the company business office. A permit may also be
issued by electronic means if requested by the individual or company applying
for the permit. A business which operates within the county may obtain a permit
on behalf of an individual or entity which operates upon the county highway
system. The overload permit may be obtained at: Randolph County Highway
Department, 325 S. Oak St., Ste. 203, Winchester, IN 47394. Telephone: 765-584-
5605. The County Highway Superintendent and/or his or her agents must exercise
sound discretion in the granting of the overload permits. Overweight permits
are to be issued on forms acceptable to the County Board of Commissioners.
After due application, and after permittee has been issued the written overload
permit from the County Highway Superintendent, any such permittee may operate
or cause to be operated upon the county highway system any lawful vehicle or
vehicle combination, having a maximum gross axle weight, with load, in excess
of the limitations of weight as set forth herein, and being otherwise lawful;
however, each and all such permittees, with each separate excess weight so
specifically permitted, shall operate and move the specifically permitted
weight loads only upon the roads and highways of the county highway system on
the route and as directed in writing by the County Highway Superintendent in
granting the written permits.
   (E)   Empty trucks are exempt from the permit process. An empty truck is
defined as a truck with standard equipment.
   (F)   The County Highway Superintendent is hereby authorized to set or
reduce speed limits when issuing permits; however, in no instance is the speed
limit to be reduced to less than 30 miles per hour.
   (G)   Any monies generated from the granting of special permits, or monies
generated as a result of fines for violation of this section shall be deposited
in a special non-reverting fund, entitled Randolph County Highway Overweight
Vehicle Fund, and the Randolph County Sheriff’s Department Highway Fund.
Randolph County Highway Overweight Vehicle Fund shall receive 90% of all
revenue generated, and the Randolph County Sheriff’s Department Highway fund
shall receive 10% of all revenue generated. The Randolph County Highway
Overweight Vehicle Fund shall be utilized specifically to maintain and preserve
the county roads in Randolph County. The Randolph County Sheriff’s Department
Highway Fund shall be used by the Sheriff’s Department for the costs of
maintaining vehicles and equipment, training, and for the cost of gasoline and
other utilities at the Sheriff’s Department.
   (H)   The Randolph County Highway Overweight Vehicle Fund created herein
shall remain in existence and remain perpetual in its terms, until amended or
terminated by subsequent ordinance enacted by the County Board of
Commissioners.
   (I)   If and when the Randolph County Highway Overweight Vehicle Fund is
terminated by ordinance, the fund balance existing, if any, at that time, and
if not otherwise provided for by ordinance, shall be transferred to the
Randolph County General Fund.
   (J)   Enforcement of this section shall be by the County Sheriff. It shall
be the duty of the County Attorney, to whom the County Sheriff shall report any
violations of this section, to cause proceedings to be commenced against the
person(s) violating the provisions of this section, and to proinal termination.
   (K)   Violation of any posted sign regulating weight limits of vehicles
utilizing public rights-of-way in unincorporated areas of Randolph County,
shall be an infraction, and, upon conviction, shall be punishable as follows:
      (1)   For a violation of a posted vehicular weight limit, court costs,
and a fine as set forth in the following schedule:
         (a)   Overweight without a special permit: zero to 4,000 pounds
overweight, $300.00.
         (b)   Over 4,000 pounds overweight: $500.00, plus $100.00 additional
fine for each 1,000) pounds or portion thereof above 4,000 pounds overweight.
         (c)   Overweight with special permit: Any owner or operator of a
vehicle which is operated on public rights-of-way in the unincorporated areas
of Randolph County, in violation of the weight limits imposed under a special
permit granted by the County Highway Superintendent, shall, upon a finding
thereof, be subject to the payment of costs and a fine pursuant to the schedule
set forth in (K)(1)(a) and (b), as if no special permit had been granted.
      (2)   Other violations. Any other violation of the terms and conditions
of a special permit granted by the County Highway Superintendent shall, upon a
finding thereof, be subject to the payment of court costs, and a fine of no
less than $300.00, and no more than $10,000.00.
(Comm. Ord. 2010-14, passed 11-15-2010)
§ 70.03 USE OF OFF-ROAD VEHICLES.
   (A)   Subject to the regulations contained within this section, and all
other applicable state and local laws, off-road vehicles (as defined by I.C.
14-8-2-185) are hereby permitted to operate on the roads and highways that are
under the jurisdiction of the Randolph County Board of Commissioners. This
section does not apply to roads and highways within the state highway system or
those roads and highways within the corporation boundaries of cities and towns
within Randolph County.
   (B)   Owners of the vehicles described herein and regulated hereby shall
register the off-road vehicle in compliance with I.C. 9-18.1-14-1; I.C. 9-18.1-
14-3; I.C. 9-18.1-14-4; and I.C. 9-18.1-14-5.
   (C)   Owners of the vehicles described herein and regulated hereby shall
comply with I.C. 14-16-1-1 et seq., as applicable.
   (D)   A person under the age of 18 years old shall comply with I.C. 9-18.1-
14-11 (Youth Helmet Law) while riding or operating an off-road vehicle.
   (E)   Any person who operates an off-road vehicle upon the roads and
highways that are under the jurisdiction of the Randolph County Board of
Commissioners, shall possess a valid driver license.
   (F)   An operator of an off-road vehicle shall have evidence of in place and
valid liability insurance issued by an insurance underwriter authorized to
issue insurance in the state of Indiana in an amount equal to or greater than
the insurance requirements set forth in I.C. 9-25-4-5 whenever the off-road
vehicle is operated upon the roads and highways under the jurisdiction of the
Board of Commissioners.
   (G)   No passengers are permitted on single seat off-road vehicles. The
number of passengers permitted at any time to simultaneously occupy an off-road
vehicle is limited to the number of manufacturer-installed passenger seats on
the vehicle.
   (H)   An operator of an off-road vehicle is permitted to cross a highway in
the state highway system, at right angles, in order to travel from one highway
under the jurisdiction of the county to another highway under the jurisdiction
of the county when the operation can be done safely.
   (I)   An off-road vehicle may not be operated between sunset and sunrise
without at least one operable headlight and one operable taillight.
   (J)   Off-road vehicles must travel in single file when operated upon road
and highways under the jurisdiction of the Board of Commissioners.
   (K)   Any law enforcement agency, including but not limited to: the Randolph
County Sheriffs Department, the Indiana State Police, Indiana Conservation
officers and Randolph County municipal police officers may enforce this
section.
(Comm. Ord. 2021-04, passed 5-17-2021)
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this traffic code for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any person who violates §
70.01(B)(1) through §
70.01(B)(3) shall be subject to a Class C infraction.
   (C)   Penalties for a violation of §
70.03 are fines up to but not exceeding the following amounts:
      (1)   Violation of first offense shall be $100.
      (2)   Violation of second and subsequent offenses shall be $250.
      (3)   Three or more violations by a single operator occurring within a
12-month period shall constitute grounds for the withdrawal of the
registrations of any vehicles covered by §
70.03 owned by that operator, subject to the owner's right to appeal such
registration withdrawal to the Randolph County Board of County Commissioners.
(Comm. Ord. 2004-18, passed 8-16-2004; Comm. Ord. 2013-20, passed 12-2-2013;
Comm. Ord. 2021-04, passed 5-17-2021)

SCHEDULE II. STOP SIGNS.
   (A)   (1)   For the purpose of convenience in this schedule, the county is
divided into four districts and the highway intersections in each district
designated as “stop” intersections, in separate sections.
      (2)   Division (B) below includes intersections south of Base Road West
and west of Base Road South.
      (3)   Division (C) below includes intersections north of Base Road West
and west of Base Road North.
      (4)   Division (D) below includes intersections north of Base Road East
and east of Base Road North.
      (5)   Division (E) below includes intersections south of Base Road East
and east of Base Road South.
   (B)   Southwest part of county.
      (1)   Base Road South shall “stop” to all recorded intersections, except:
Traffic on C.R. 40 S shall “stop” for traffic on Western Avenue entering and
exiting the Golf Club area
Traffic on Base Road South shall “stop” for road C.R. 800 S
Traffic on C.R. 1000 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 875 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 850 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 300 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 400 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 600 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 700 S shall “stop” to Base Road South
The intersection of Base Road South and C.R. 1100 S (the Wayne-Randolph County
line) shall be a “4-way stop” for all traffic
Traffic on Base Road South shall “stop” for traffic on 950 S
The intersection of Base Road South and C.R. 500 S shall be a “4- way stop”
 
      (2)   All marked intersections of C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road,
from the corporate limits of Winchester to the Wayne County line, cross traffic
shall “stop” for traffic on C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road, except:
 
Traffic on C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road shall “stop” at intersection with
C.R. 800 S
The intersection of C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road and C.R. 950 S shall be a
“4-way stop”
Traffic on C.R. 1100 S shall “stop” at intersection with C.R. 100 W
Traffic on C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road shall “stop” at intersection of
C.R. 100 W and/or Bloomingsport Road and Huntsville Road
Traffic on C.R. 40 S shall “stop” to traffic on Huntsville Road at C.R. 65 W
Traffic on C.R. 300 S shall “stop” for traffic on Base Road South
Traffic on C.R. 150 S shall “stop” for traffic on Huntsville Road
 
      (3)   All marked intersections of C.R. 200 W, south of Base Road West to
Wayne County line, cross traffic shall “stop” to traffic on C.R. 200 W, except:

Traffic on C.R. 200 W shall “stop” to traffic on Huntsville Road
Traffic on C.R. 200 W shall “stop” for traffic on C.R. 150 S

Traffic on C.R. 200 W shall “stop” to traffic on C.R. 50 S
Traffic on C.R. 200 W shall “stop” to traffic on C.R. 1100 S

Wayne County line, cross traffic shall “stop” to traffic on C.R. 300 W, except:
Traffic on C.R. 300 W shall “stop” at intersection with C.R. 800 S
The intersection of C.R. 300 W and C.R. 950 S shall be a “4-way stop”

Road.

terminating on C.R. 425 S at its intersection with C.R. 300 W shall “stop” to

950 S, cross traffic shall “stop” to traffic on C.R. 400 W, except:

stop”

   (G)   The County Highway Map with recommended “stop” intersections indicated
thereon, as submitted to the Board of County Commissioners by the County
Highway Supervisor, shall be and hereby is adopted as the traffic control plan
of the county and the same shall remain on file in the office of the County
Highway Supervisor.
   (H)   The County Highway Supervisor is hereby directed to erect or cause to
be erected “stop” signs, for traffic control on county highways in conformity
with the traffic control plan of the county as shown on the County Highway Map
and as directed in divisions (B), (C), (D) and (E) above.
   (I)   At all unmarked intersections of county roads, traffic approaching any
intersections from the right, shall have preference over the traffic
approaching from the left.
   (J)   The operators of all vehicles approaching any highway intersection in
the county shall “stop” at the right-of-way in the intersection, as indicated
by warning signs posted at the intersections, or by designated section above,
before entering the same.
   (K)   Any person violating any provisions of this schedule shall be guilty
of a Class C infraction.

divided into four districts and the highway bridges in each district
designated, bridge weight limits of two-, three-, four-, five-, six-, seven-,
eight-, nine-, ten-, 11-, 12-, 13- and 14-ton, in separate sections.

and west of Base Road South.

and west of Base Road North.

and east of Base Road North.

and east of Base Road South.

      (10)   Weight limit signs of “11-TON” shall be posted on the following
bridges:
 
Bridge Location
259    Located on C.R. 25 E at C.R. 920 S
 
   (F)   The County Highway Supervisor is directed to cause the proper weight
limit signs to be erected in a conspicuous place at each end of the bridge
structure and the signs shall measure 18 inches by 24 inches and be legible
from a distance of 50 feet and is hereby adopted as the Bridge Traffic Control
Plan of the county and the same shall remain on file in the office of the
County Highway Supervisor. Also, a copy shall be in the code of ordinances in
the County Auditor’s Office.
   (G)   The County Highway Supervisor is hereby directed to erect or cause to
be erected, weight limit signs of two-, three-, four-, five-, six-, seven-,
eight-, nine-, ten-, 11-, 12-, 13- and 14-tons for weight control on county
bridges in conformity with the Bridge Control Plan of the county as shown on
the County Highway Map, as directed in divisions (B), (C), (D) and (E) above.
   (H)   Any person violating any provision of this schedule shall be guilty of
committing a Class C infraction.
(1985 Code, Ch. 12, Art. 1) (Comm. Ord. 85-(3), passed 9-3-1985)
